5.3.2
Decoding Algorithm

\displaystyle\mbox{find}\hskip 9.24994pt~~\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}~\text{on}~{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p],E}\text{ and alphabet }\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}

subject to

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\text{(i)}~~\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}\text{is a }p\text{-tuple of pseudocodewords of SoS-degree }t

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\text{(ii)}~~\forall i\in[p],~\text{the constraint }\Delta_{R}(g,{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})<\frac{k-1}{k}(1-\rho-\varepsilon)\text{ is respected by }\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\text{(iii)}~\forall F\subseteq E\text{ such that }|F|\leq\frac{t-2pd}{2},\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{[p]\times F}\text{ and }\forall~i,i^{\prime}\in[p]\text{ with }i\neq i^{\prime},

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},{\mathbf{Z}}_{i^{\prime}})-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p],F}=\sigma\right\}}^{2}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},{\mathbf{Z}}_{i^{\prime}})-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p],F}=\sigma\right\}}^{2}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},{\mathbf{Z}}_{i^{\prime}})-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p],F}=\sigma\right\}}^{2}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},{\mathbf{Z}}_{i^{\prime}})-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p],F}=\sigma\right\}}^{2}\right]}\geq 0

Table 1:
\mathrm{SDP}(p,t)

5.21
.

Let
k\geq 1
be an integer and let
\varepsilon>0
. Let
{{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{AEL}}}
be a
code obtained using the AEL construction using
(G,{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out}},{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}})
, where
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}
is
(\delta_{0},k,\varepsilon/2)
average-radius list decodable with erasures, and
G
is a
(n,d,\lambda)
-expander.
Suppose that
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out}}
is unique decodable from radius
\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}
in time
{\mathcal{T}}(n)
.

If
\lambda\leq\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}}{12{k}^{k}}\cdot\varepsilon
, then there is a deterministic algorithm that takes as input
g\in(\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{d})^{R}
, runs in time
{\mathcal{T}}(n)+n^{O\left(\frac{d\cdot k^{3k}|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|^{3kd}}{(\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}})^{2}\cdot\varepsilon^{2}}\right)}
, and outputs the list
{\mathcal{L}}(g,\frac{k-1}{k}(\delta_{0}-\varepsilon))
.

Algorithm 5.22

(List Decoding algorithm up to
\frac{k-1}{k}(\delta_{0}-\varepsilon)
)
.

Input

k
,
g\in(\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{d})^{R}

Output

List of codewords
{\mathcal{L}}\left(g,\frac{k-1}{k}\left(\delta_{0}-\varepsilon\right)\right)

1.

Initialize
{\mathcal{L}}=\{\}
,
t=2kd\cdot\left(1+\frac{144k^{2k}|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|^{3kd}}{(\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}})^{2}\cdot\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
.

2.

Let
p^{*}
be the largest
p\in[k]
such that
\mathrm{SDP}(p,t)
is feasible.

3.

For every
F\subseteq E
with
|F|\leq\frac{t-2kd}{2}
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{F}
with
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma\right\}}\right]>0
:

•

For
j=1
to
p^{*}
:

–

Construct an ensemble of distributions
{\mathcal{D}}=\{{\mathcal{D}}_{{\ell}}\}_{{\ell}\in L}
, with each
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\ell}}
over
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}
by using the local distribution induced by the conditioned
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[~\cdot\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma\right]
over the set
\{j\}\times N({\ell})
, or equivalently, over variables
{\mathbf{Z}}_{j,{\ell}}
.

–

h\leftarrow\text{Decode-from-distributions}\left({\mathcal{D}}\right)
.

–

If
\Delta_{R}(g,h)<\frac{k-1}{k}(\delta_{0}-\varepsilon)
, add
h
to
{\mathcal{L}}
.

4.

Return
{\mathcal{L}}
.

Proof.

Algorithm

5.22
describes this algorithm. In the rest of the proof, we argue the correctness of this algorithm.

We first start with a lemma that readily follows from
Theorem

5.13
.

5.23
.

If
\lambda\leq\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}}{12k^{k}}\cdot\varepsilon
, and
t\geq 2kd\cdot\left(1+\frac{144k^{2k}|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|^{3kd}}{(\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}})^{2}\cdot\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
, then the
\mathrm{SDP}(k,t)
is infeasible.

In other words, the lemma says that
p^{*}<k
, and that
\mathrm{SDP}(p^{*}+1,t)
is infeasible. Let
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[\cdot\right]
be the
p^{*}
-tuple of pseudocodewords found by
\mathrm{SDP}(p^{*},t)
.

5.24
.

For any
h\in{\mathcal{L}}\left(g,\frac{k-1}{k}(1-\rho-\varepsilon)\right)
,
there exists an
i\in[p^{*}]
, a set
F\subseteq E
with
|F|\leq\frac{t-2kd}{2}
, and a
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{[p^{*}]\times F}
with
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma\right\}}\right]>0
, such that

\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[\Delta_{L}(h,{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma\right]\leq\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}.

This lemma proves the correctness of the algorithm, since then
h
will be added to
{\mathcal{L}}
in
Step
3
, by
Lemma

5.19
.

Proof of
Lemma

5.24
.

To show this, we will construct a
(p^{*}+1)
-tuple of pseudocodewords, say
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[\cdot\right]
, and use its infeasibility for
\mathrm{SDP}(p^{*}+1,t)
.

Recall that
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[\cdot\right]
is an SoS relaxation over the variables
{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}]\times E}
,
while
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[\cdot\right]
will be an SoS relaxation over the variables
{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}+1]\times E}
.

To describe the new
(p^{*}+1)
-tuple of pseudocodewords, we explicitly specify the corresponding pseudoexpectation operator
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[\cdot\right]
.
Let
P
be an arbitrary polynomial of degree
t
over
{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}+1],E}
. We define a new polynomial
P_{h}
over
{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],E}
by assigning the variables
{\mathbf{Z}}_{p^{*}+1,e}
to be
h_{e}
for every
e\in E
.
Then, we define
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[\cdot\right]
using the following

\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[P({\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}+1],E})\right]=\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[P_{h}({\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],E})\right]

This is well-defined since the degree of
P_{h}
cannot be more than
t
.

By optimality of
p^{*}
,
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[\cdot\right]
must be infeasible for
\mathrm{SDP}(p^{*}+1,t)
. It can be verified that
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[\cdot\right]
is a valid
(p^{*}+1)
-tuple of pseudocodewords. Further, the constraints

\Delta_{R}(g,{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})<\frac{k-1}{k}(\delta_{0}-\varepsilon)

are respected for all
i\in[p^{*}]
. This is because

\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[P({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\cdot\left(\Delta_{R}(g,{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})-\frac{k-1}{k}(\delta_{0}-\varepsilon)\right)\right]=\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[P_{h}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\cdot\left(\Delta_{R}(g,{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})-\frac{k-1}{k}(\delta_{0}-\varepsilon)\right)\right]<0

Further, for
i=p^{*}+1
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[P({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\cdot\left(\Delta_{R}(g,{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})-\frac{k-1}{k}(\delta_{0}-\varepsilon)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[P_{h}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\cdot\left(\Delta_{R}(g,h)-\frac{k-1}{k}(\delta_{0}-\varepsilon)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\Delta_{R}(g,h)-\frac{k-1}{k}(\delta_{0}-\varepsilon)\right)\cdot\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[P_{h}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle<0

Therefore, the infeasibility must be due to constraints of type (iii) in the SDP. In other words, there exist

1.

a set
F\subseteq E
with
|F|\leq\frac{t-2(p^{*}+1)d}{2}
,

2.

a
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{[p^{*}+1]\times F}

, and

3.

a pair
i,i^{\prime}\in[p^{*}+1]
with
i\neq i^{\prime}

such that

\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},{\mathbf{Z}}_{i^{\prime}})-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}+1],F}=\sigma\right\}}^{2}\right]<0

Case I.
i,i^{\prime}\in[p^{*}]
:

In this case,

\displaystyle 0~>

\displaystyle~\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},{\mathbf{Z}}_{i^{\prime}})-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}+1],F}=\sigma\right\}}\right]

\displaystyle=~~

\displaystyle\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},{\mathbf{Z}}_{i^{\prime}})-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma_{1}\right\}}\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{p^{*}+1,F}=\sigma_{2}\right\}}\right]

\displaystyle=~~

\displaystyle\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},{\mathbf{Z}}_{i^{\prime}})-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma_{1}\right\}}\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{h_{F}=\sigma_{2}\right\}}\right]

\displaystyle=~~

\displaystyle{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{h_{F}=\sigma_{2}\right\}}\cdot\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},{\mathbf{Z}}_{i^{\prime}})-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma_{1}\right\}}\right]

Because of the strict inequality with
0
, it must be the case that
{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{h_{F}=\sigma_{2}\right\}}=1
, and the resulting equation contradicts the feasibility of
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[\cdot\right]
. So this case cannot happen.

Case II. Without loss of generality,
i^{\prime}=p^{*}+1
.

In this case,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle~>~\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},{\mathbf{Z}}_{p^{*}+1})-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}+1],F}=\sigma\right\}}\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*}+1)}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},{\mathbf{Z}}_{p^{*}+1})-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma_{1}\right\}}\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{p^{*}+1,F}=\sigma_{2}\right\}}\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},h)-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma_{1}\right\}}\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{h_{F}=\sigma_{2}\right\}}\right]

\displaystyle={{\mathbf{1}}\left\{h_{F}=\sigma_{2}\right\}}\cdot\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},h)-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma_{1}\right\}}\right]

Again, as before we must have
{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{h_{F}=\sigma_{2}\right\}}=1
, so that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[\left(\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},h)-\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}\right)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma_{1}\right\}}\right]<0

(5)

Finally, we may also assume
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}+1],F}=\sigma\right\}}\right]>0
. If not, then

\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},h)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma_{1}\right\}}\right]<0

which is impossible since
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[P({\mathbf{Z}})\right]\geq 0
for all polynomials that are sum of squares of polynomials. It is easy to verify that
\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},h)\cdot{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma_{1}\right\}}
is a sum of squares of polynomials.

Therefore, dividing
Eq.

5
by
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}\left\{{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}+1],F}=\sigma\right\}}\right]
, we get that the set
F
and the assignment
\sigma_{1}\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{[p^{*}]\times F}
have the property that

\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(p^{*})}\left[\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},h)\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[p^{*}],F}=\sigma_{1}\right]\leq\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}

as needed.

Proof of
Lemma

5.23
.

Suppose
\mathrm{SDP}(k,t)
is feasible with
t=2kd\cdot\left(1+\frac{144k^{2k}|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|^{3kd}}{(\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}})^{2}\cdot\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
, so that there exists a
k
-tuple of pseudocodewords
\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}
that satisfies all the constraints in the SDP.
Let
\eta=\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}}{12k^{k}}\cdot\varepsilon
.

Then
t\geq 2kd\cdot\left(1+\frac{|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|^{3kd}}{\eta^{2}}\right)
, so that
Lemma

A.4
says that there exists a set
S\subseteq E
of size at most
d\cdot\frac{|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|^{3kd}}{\eta^{2}}
such that

\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(S)}\left[\cdot\right]:=\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[~\cdot\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],S}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[~\cdot\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],S}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[~\cdot\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],S}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[~\cdot\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],S}\right]}

is
\eta
-good.

Further, since the constraint
\Delta_{R}(g,{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})<\frac{k-1}{k}(1-\rho-\varepsilon)
is
respected
by
\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}
, we can also conclude that for all
i\in[k]
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(S)}\left[\Delta_{R}(g,{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})\right]<\frac{k-1}{k}(1-\rho-\varepsilon)

(6)

Moreover, for any
i,i^{\prime}\in[k]
with
i\neq i^{\prime}
,

where
{\mathcal{D}}_{[k]\times S}
is the local distribution on
{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k]\times S}
according to
\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}
. This means that

\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(S)}\left[\Delta_{L}({\mathbf{Z}}_{i},{\mathbf{Z}}_{i^{\prime}})\right]\geq\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}

Since
\lambda\leq\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}}{12k^{k}}\cdot\varepsilon
and
\eta\leq\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}}{12k^{k}}\cdot\varepsilon
, we can apply
Theorem

5.13
to
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(S)}\left[\cdot\right]
with
\beta=\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}
and get

\sum_{i\in[k]}\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(S)}\left[\Delta_{R}(g,{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})\right]\geq\frac{k-1}{k}(1-\rho-\varepsilon)+\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(S)}\left[\Delta_{R}(g,{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k]})\right]\geq\frac{k-1}{k}(1-\rho-\varepsilon)

which is contradicted by
Eq.

6
.

Finally, we instantiate
Theorem

5.21
with unique decodable outer codes to obtain codes that can be efficiently decoded up to the list decoding capacity.

5.25
.

For every
\rho,\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, there exist explicit inner codes
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}
and an infinite family explicit codes
{{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{AEL}}}\subseteq({\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{d})^{n}
obtained via the AEL construction that satisfy:

1.

\rho({{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{AEL}}})\geq\rho

2.

For any
g\in({\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{d})^{n}
and any
{\mathcal{H}}\subseteq{{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{AEL}}}
with
\lvert{\mathcal{H}}\rvert\leq k
that

\sum_{h\in{\mathcal{H}}}\Delta(g,h)~\geq~(\lvert{\mathcal{H}}\rvert-1)\cdot(1-\rho-\varepsilon)\,.

3.

The alphabet size
q^{d}
of the code
{{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{AEL}}}
can be taken to be
2^{O(k^{3k}/\varepsilon^{9})}
.

4.

{{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{AEL}}}
can be decoded from radius
\frac{k-1}{k}(1-\rho-\varepsilon)
in time
n^{2^{O(k^{4k}/\varepsilon^{10})}}
with a list of size at most
k-1
.

Proof.

The first three properties can be ensured by instantiating the AEL amplification procedure as in
Corollary

3.7
. We briefly recall the choices in that instantiation.

•

We picked a graph
G
with
d=O(k^{2k}/\varepsilon^{8})
and
\lambda\leq\varepsilon^{4}/(2^{21}k^{k})
.

•

We picked
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{d}
to be a code with rate
\rho_{\mathrm{in}}=\rho+\varepsilon/4
, which is
(1-\rho,k,\varepsilon/2)
average-radius list decodable with erasures. The alphabet size
|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|
for
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}
was
2^{O(k+1/\varepsilon)}
.

•

Finally, we picked
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out}}\subseteq(\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\cdot d})^{n}
be an outer code with rate
\rho_{\mathrm{out}}=1-\varepsilon/4
and distance (say)
\delta_{\mathrm{out}}=\varepsilon^{3}/2^{15}
. We note that this code can also be decoded from a radius
\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}=\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{2^{17}}

[
Zém01
,
GRS23
]
in linear time.

The choice of
\lambda
above is sufficient to ensure
\lambda\leq\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}}}{12k^{k}}\cdot\varepsilon
, and so we can use
Theorem

5.21
to conclude that there is a deterministic algorithm, that takes as input
g\in(\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{d})^{R}
, runs in time
n^{O\left(\frac{d\cdot k^{3k}|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|^{3kd}}{(\delta_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{dec}})^{2}\cdot\varepsilon^{2}}\right)}
, and outputs the list
{\mathcal{L}}\left(g,\frac{k-1}{k}\left(1-\rho-\varepsilon\right)\right)
. With the above choice of parameters, the exponent of
n
in the runtime becomes
2^{O(\frac{k^{4k}}{\varepsilon^{10}})}
.

Appendix A
Auxiliary SoS claims

This appendix is adapted from
[
JST23
]
to prove properties about
\eta
-good pseudocodewords and how one can obtain them via random conditioning. The key change is that we need these notions for
k
-tuples of pseudocodewords. Finally, we prove a generalization of the expander mixing lemma for such
k
-tuples of pseudowords.

A.2
Conditioning reduces variance

We next show how to obtain the
\eta
-good property by randomly conditioning the
k
-tuple of pseudocodewords. This is a straightforward generalization of the argument in
[
JST23
]
, which can be seen as the
k=1
case.

Just as in
[
JST23
]
, we start with a lemma from
[
BRS11
]
that quantifies the decrease in variance of the local distribution corresponding to a set
S
when conditioning on another small set
T
, in terms of covariances between the two sets
S
and
T
.

A.2
[
BRS11
, Lemma 5.2]
.

Let
\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}
be a
k
-tuple of pseudocodewords of degree
t
. Let
S,T
be subsets of
[k]\times E
of size at most
t/2
each. Then,

\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{T}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{T}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{T}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{T}\right]}\leq\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S}\right]}-\frac{1}{|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|^{|T|}}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{S}\\
\beta\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{T}\end{subarray}}\frac{\left(\mathchoice{{\tildecov}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S,\alpha},{\mathbf{Z}}_{T,\beta}\right]}{{\tildecov}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S,\alpha},{\mathbf{Z}}_{T,\beta}\right]}{{\tildecov}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S,\alpha},{\mathbf{Z}}_{T,\beta}\right]}{{\tildecov}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{S,\alpha},{\mathbf{Z}}_{T,\beta}\right]}\right)^{2}}{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{T,\beta}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{T,\beta}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{T,\beta}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{T,\beta}\right]}}

Now we use this lemma to track the decrease in average (pseudo-)variance
\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}\in L}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\in L}\left[\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\in L}\left[\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\in L}\left[\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}\right]}
when conditioning on
{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({r})}
for a random
{r}\in R
.

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\underset{{r}\in R}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}\in \displaystyle=~\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}\in L}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\in L}\left[\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\in L}\left[\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\in L}\left[\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathrm{Var}}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N({\ell})}\right]}\right]}-\frac{1}{|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|^{3kd}}\cdot\left(\mathchoice{\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}{\ell}\in L\\

Now that we can use average correlation to quantify the decrease in variance, we show that for a
k
-tuple of pseudocodewords with high-enough degree, there always exists a constant-sized subset of
R
such that randomly conditioning on this set gives an
\eta
-good
k
-tuple of pseudocodewords.

A.4
.

Let
\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}
be a
k
-tuple of pseudocodewords of degree
t\geq 2kd\left(1+\frac{|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|^{3kd}}{\eta^{2}}\right)
. Then there exists a set
S\subseteq E
of size at most
d\cdot\frac{|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|^{3kd}}{\eta^{2}}
such that the conditioned pseudocodeword
\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],S}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],S}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],S}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],S}\right]}
is
\eta
-good.

Proof.

For an integer
C>0
to be chosen later, consider the following sequence of pseudocodewords, obtained by sequentially conditioning on
{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}}
for a random
{r}
. For

, define

We have that

1\geq\Psi(0)\geq\Psi(1)\geq\cdots\Psi(C+1)\geq 0

Therefore, there must exist a

, such that

\Psi(c^{*})-\Psi(c^{*}+1)\leq\frac{1}{C+1}<\frac{1}{C}=\frac{\eta^{2}}{|\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}|^{3kd}}

, let us denote

\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}}\right]\right]}-\mathchoice{\underset{{r}_{c^{*}+1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}_{c^{*}+1}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}_{c^{*}+1}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}_{c^{*}+1}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}_{c^{*}+1}}\right]\right]}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}_{c^{*}+1}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}_{c^{*}+1}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}_{c^{*}+1}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}_{c^{*}+1}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}_{c^{*}+1}}\right]\right]}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}_{c^{*}+1}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Var}}^ to
\widetilde{\ExpOp}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[\cdot\right]
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell},{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Cov}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}},{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Cov}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}},{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Cov}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}},{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Cov}}^{(R^{\prime})}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}},{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}}\right]\right]}\leq\eta

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell},{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Cov}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}},{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N(R^{\prime})}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Cov}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}},{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N(R^{\prime})}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Cov}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}},{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N(R^{\prime})}\right]\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[\widetilde{\operatorname{Cov}}\left[{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{\ell}},{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],{r}}\;|\;{\mathbf{Z}}_{[k],N(R^{\prime})}\right]\right]}\leq\eta

Therefore, the set
S=N(R^{\prime})
proves the lemma.

A.3
Expander Mixing Lemma

A.5
EML for pseudoexpectations.

Let
\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\cdot\right]}
be a
k
-tuple of pseudocodewords of degree at least
2kd
. Let
G=(L,R,E)
be a bipartite
\lambda
-expander. Recall that
{\ell}\sim{r}
if and only if
({\ell},{r})
is an edge in
G
.

Let
\{X_{{\ell}}\}_{{\ell}\in L}
be a collection of
kd
-local functions on
\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{[k]\times E}
, such that
X_{{\ell}}(f)
only depends
f_{[k],{\ell}}
. Likewise, let
\{Y_{{r}}\}_{{r}\in R}
be a collection of
kd
-local functions on
\Sigma_{\mathrm{in}}^{[k]\times E}
, such that
Y_{{r}}(f)
only depends
f_{[k],{r}}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}\sim{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\sim{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\sim{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\sim{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}-\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell},{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}\sim{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\sim{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\sim{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\sim{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}-\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell},{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}\sim{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\sim{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\sim{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\sim{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}-\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell},{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}\sim{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\sim{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\sim{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}\sim{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}-\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell},{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell},{r}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})\cdot Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]}\right]}\right\rvert\leq\lambda\cdot\sqrt{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{\ell}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\ell}}\left[X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}\right]}}\cdot\sqrt{\mathchoice{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}\right]}{{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathchoice{\underset{{r}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{r}}\left[Y_{{r}}({\mathbf{Z}})^{2}\right]}\right]}}

Proof.

Let
A_{G}
be the
L\times R
normalized biadjacency matrix of
G
, so that

A_{G}({\ell},{r})=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{d},\qquad({\ell},{r})\in E\\
0,\qquad({\ell},{r})\not\in E\end{cases}

The singular value decomposition for
A_{G}
can be written as

A_{G}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}u_{i}v_{i}^{T}

with
\sigma_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\sigma_{n}\geq 0
, and
\{u_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
,
\{v_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
being two orthonormal bases of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Moreover, since the graph is regular,
\sigma_{1}=1
and
u_{1}=v_{1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\mathbf{1}}
.

Let
X({\mathbf{Z}})
be the vector-valued local function defined as the
n
-dimensional vector with coordinates corresponding to
{\ell}\in L
, and the respective entry being
X_{{\ell}}({\mathbf{Z}})
. Likewise, let
Y({\mathbf{Z}})
be the vector-valued local function so that coordinates correspond to
{r}\in R
, and the entries being
